Provides strategy-based cleaning operations including deduplication, missing value imputation, text processing, and outlier handling through a configurable pipeline.\n\n## Core Components\n\n### CleaningStrategies\nCollection of static methods for specific cleaning operations:\n\n#### Deduplication\n- `remove_duplicates(df, subset=None)` - Remove duplicate rows given the context of the prompt or the context of the dataset, Carefully evaluate if it still makes sense to allow duplicates exist in the downstream analysis. You should always check the data and remove them based on the logic of meta data and downstream goal.\n\n#### Missing Value Handling\nLogic: If the column is not critical to affect the dowstream task and the cost of dropping the whole line is not neccessary, you should try to impute them, otherwise you can drop.\n- `drop_missing(df, columns)` - Drop rows with missing critical values (that will affect the downstream task's peformance) in specified columns.\n- `impute_median(df, columns)` - Impute with median for numerical columns if the column is numerical data\n- `impute_knn(df, target_features, n_neighbors=5)` - KNN-based imputation such that the target column value potentially can be inferred by other columns. I.e. you have to carefully see the semantic and logical relationship between columns in terms of real life cases and make decisions to use this method for better data imputation performance.\n- `impute_mode(df, columns)` - Impute with mode for categorical columns if the target column is relatively independent to other columns and should just be filled with an acceptable value.\n\n#### Text Processing\n- `process_text(df, columns, operation)` - Text operations: some columns might need conduct text related processing to get numbers or remove unnecessary content, you need to dertermine if this is needed based on preview the column values regarding the down stream goal. (extract_numbers, clean_whitespace, extract_email, lowercase, remove_special)\n\n#### Outlier Handling\nLogic: Again you need to make sure if removing outliers is costly or not in terms of the sample size or critical columns under your investigation. Keep the rows by replacing the outlier value with a more reasonable value, or simply remove the whole row in one of the two ways if it barely has any impact.\n- `cap_outliers_iqr(df, columns, multiplier=1.5)` - Winsorization using IQR to keep reasonable signals of the data;\n- `remove_outliers_iqr(df, columns, multiplier=1.5)` - Remove outliers using IQR\n- `remove_outliers_zscore(df, columns, threshold=3.0)` - Remove outliers using Z-score\n\n### DataCleaningPipeline\nOrchestrates multiple cleaning steps with logging and progress tracking.\n\n## Usage\n\n```python\nfrom data_cleaning import CleaningStrategies, DataCleaningPipeline\n\n# Create pipeline\npipeline = DataCleaningPipeline(name=\"Transaction Data\")\n\n# Add cleaning steps\npipeline.add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.remove_duplicates,\n    subset=['customer_id', 'order_date'],\n    description=\"Remove duplicate orders\"\n).add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.drop_missing,\n    columns=['customer_id', 'amount'],\n    description=\"Drop rows with missing critical fields\"\n).add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.impute_knn,\n    target_features={\n        'income': {'features': ['age', 'education'], 'type': 'numeric'}\n    },\n    description=\"KNN imputation for income\"\n).add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.cap_outliers_iqr,\n    columns=['amount'],\n    multiplier=1.5,\n    description=\"Cap price outliers\"\n)\n\n# Execute pipeline\ndf_clean = pipeline.execute(raw_df, verbose=True)\n\n# Get execution log\nlog_df = pipeline.get_log()\n```\n\n## Input\n- Any tabular DataFrame requiring cleaning\n\n## Output\n- Cleaned DataFrame with execution log available\n\n## Best Practices\n- Chain multiple steps using method chaining\n- Use verbose=True to monitor pipeline progress\n- Review execution log for quality assurance\n- Extend CleaningStrategies with domain-specific methods as needed\n\n\nARGUMENTS: /app/data/survey_dirty.csv"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"389924b5-70f7-41f8-8eb9-378490f5b7ce","uuid":"65a57805-086c-4a73-af70-04b2487057be","isSynthetic":true}

purchases['Quantity'].fillna(1)\npurchases = purchases[purchases['Quantity'] > 0]\n\n# Cap outliers in price (use IQR method)\nQ1 = purchases['Purchase Price Per Unit'].quantile(0.25)\nQ3 = purchases['Purchase Price Per Unit'].quantile(0.75)\nIQR = Q3 - Q1\nupper_bound = Q3 + 1.5 * IQR\npurchases.loc[purchases['Purchase Price Per Unit'] > upper_bound, 'Purchase Price Per Unit'] = 'Category'])['Total_Spend'].sum().reset_index()\ndaily_category['Date'] = pd.to_datetime(daily_category['Date'])\n\nprint(f\"\\nDaily category data: {daily_category.shape}\")\nprint(f\"Date range: {daily_category['Date'].min()} to {daily_category['Date'].max()}\")\nprint(f\"Unique categories: {daily_category['Category'].nunique()}\")\n\n# Use Prophet-like approach to detect anomalies\nfrom scipy import stats\n\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(category_data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly index for a category.\n    Returns a value from -100 to 100:\n    - 100: very unusual increase\n    - 0: normal spending\n    - -100: unusual decrease\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(category_data) < 10:\n        return 0\n\n    # Separate baseline (Jan-Feb 2020) from treatment (Mar 2020)\n    baseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n    baseline = category_data[(category_data['Date'] >= baseline_start) & (category_data['Date'] <= baseline_end)]\n    treatment = category_data[(category_data['Date'] >= treatment_start) & (category_data['Date'] <= treatment_end)]\n\n    if len(baseline) < 3 or len(treatment) < 3:\n        return 0\n\n    baseline_mean = baseline['Total_Spend'].mean()\n    baseline_std = baseline['Total_Spend'].std()\n    treatment_mean = treatment['Total_Spend'].mean()\n\n    if baseline_std == 0:\n        return 0\n\n    # Calculate z-score for treatment period\n    z_score = (treatment_mean - baseline_mean) / baseline_std\n\n    # Convert to -100 to 100 scale\n    anomaly_index = np.clip(z_score * 20, -100, 100)\n\n    return anomaly_index\n\n# Calculate anomaly index for each category\nanomaly_scores = []\n\nfor category in daily_category['Category'].unique():\n    if category == 'Unknown':\n        continue\n\n    cat_data = daily_category[daily_category['Category'] == category].copy()\n    anomaly_idx = calculate_anomaly_index(cat_data)\n\n    anomaly_scores.append({\n        'Category': category,\n        'Anomaly_Index': anomaly_idx\n    })\n\nanomaly_df = pd.DataFrame(anomaly_scores).sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False)\nanomaly_df.to_csv('/app/output/category_anomaly_index.csv', index=False)\n\nprint(f\"\\nTop 10 SURGE categories (highest anomaly index):\")\nprint(anomaly_df.head(10)[['Category', 'Anomaly_Index']])\n\nprint(f\"\\nTop 10 SLUMP categories (lowest anomaly index):\")\nprint(anomaly_df.tail(10)[['Category', 'Anomaly_Index']])\n\n# Get top categories for analysis\ntop_surge_categories = anomaly_df.head(10)['Category'].tolist()\ntop_slump_categories = anomaly_df.tail(10)['Category'].tolist()\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 80)\nprint(\"STEP 3: FEATURE ENGINEERING ON DEMOGRAPHIC DATA\")\nprint(\"=\" * 80)\n\n# Create engineered features from survey data\nsurvey_eng = survey.copy()\n\n# Feature engineering functions\ndef extract_age_numeric(age_str):\n    \"\"\"Extract numeric age from range string\"\"\"\n    mapping = {\n        '18 - 24 years': - 0: normal spending\n    - -100: unusual decrease\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(category_data) < 10:\n        return 0\n\n    # Separate baseline (Jan-Feb 2020) from treatment (Mar 2020)\n    baseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /app/.claude/skills/did_causal_analysis\n\n# Difference-in-Differences Framework\n\nDiD framework for causal inference with automatic method selection (Multivariate vs Univariate) based on sample size.\n\n## Methodology\n\n### Intensive Margin\n- **Definition**: How much behavior changed among participants (continuous outcome)\n- **Data Structure**: Sparse data - only entities that participated (made purchases, had events, etc.)\n- **Outcome**: Continuous (e.g., spending amount per purchaser)\n- **Key Point**: Do NOT include non-participants or create complete grids - only analyze actual participants\n\n### Extensive Margin  \n- **Definition**: How many people changed behavior (binary 0/1 outcome)\n- **Data Structure**: Complete panel - all entities × all periods (includes non-participants)\n- **Outcome**: Binary (0/1 indicating participation)\n- **Key Point**: MUST include non-participants to measure adoption ratess\n\n### Regression Methods\n\nBoth methods use: `Y = α + βX + γPost + δ(X × Post) + ε` where δ is the DiD estimate.\n\n**Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD**\n- Estimates ALL features simultaneously in one regression\n- Controls for confounding between features\n- Used when: `n ≥ k × min_sample_ratio` (default: 10k samples minimum)\n\n**Univariate DiD**\n- Separate regression for EACH feature\n- More robust with small samples\n- Used when: Insufficient sample size, or as fallback\n\n**CRITICAL**: Both use statsmodels regression to provide p-values for statistical inference.\n\n## ⚠️ CRITICAL: Data Preparation Requirements\n\n### Intensive Margin - Sparse Data (Purchasers/Participants Only)\n\n**CORRECT approach** - Only aggregate actual transactions:\n```python\n# Group actual transactions by entity-period\nintensive_df = transactions.groupby(['entity_id', 'period'])['value'].sum().reset_index()\n# Result: Only rows where events occurred (e.g., 3,404 rows for active participants)\n```\n\n**Why this matters**: Intensive margin measures \"how much MORE did participants engage\", not \"did non-participants start engaging\". Including zeros for non-participants dilutes the effect and changes the research question.\n\n### Extensive Margin - Complete Panel (All Users)\n\n**CORRECT approach** - Create complete entity × group × period grid:\n```python\n# Create all possible combinations\nfrom itertools import product\nall_entities = entities_df['entity_id'].unique()\nall_groups = target_groups  # e.g., categories, regions, products\nall_periods = ['baseline', 'treatment']\n\ncomplete_grid = pd.DataFrame(\n    list(product(all_entities, all_groups, all_periods)),\n    columns=['entity_id', 'period']\n)\n\n# Mark who participated (1) vs who didn't (0)\nevents_lookup = transactions[['entity_id',  'period']].drop_duplicates()\nevents_lookup['participated'] = 1\n\nextensive_df = complete_grid.merge(events_lookup, how='left')\nextensive_df['participated'] = extensive_df['participated'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n# Result: All entities × groups × periods with binary outcome\n```\n\n**Why this matters**: Extensive margin measures \"did the participation rate increase\", which requires knowing both who participated AND who didn't participate.\n\n## API\n### Constructor Parameters `DIDAnalyzer(min_sample_ratio=10, significance_level=0.05, min_group_size=1)`\n- `min_sample_ratio=10`: Samples-to-features ratio for multivariate DiD\n- `significance_level=0.05`: P-value threshold 0.05-0.1 based on the context and requirement\n- `min_group_size=1`: Min observations per group for univariate DiD\n\n\n### `intensive_margin(df, features, value_col, top_n=None, sort_by='estimate', asc=False)`\nAnalyzes **intensive margin**: how much behavior changed among participants.\n\n**Input Data**: Sparse entity-period data (only participants)\n- `df`: Sparse DataFrame - only entities with participation\n- `features`: List of demographic column names\n- `value_col`: Continuous outcome column (e.g., 'amount', 'spending')\n- `top_n`: Top N features (None = all)\n- `sort_by`: 'estimate' or 'p_value'\n- `asc`: True for ascending, False for descending\n\n**Returns**: List of `{\"feature\": str, \"did_estimate\": float, \"p_value\": float, \"method\": str}`\n\n### `extensive_margin(df, features, value_col='purchased', top_n=None, sort_by='estimate', asc=False)`\nAnalyzes **extensive margin**: how many people changed behavior.\n\n**Input Data**: Complete entity-period panel (all entities including non-participants)\n- `df`: Complete DataFrame - ALL entities × ALL periods (includes non-participants)\n- `features`: List of demographic column names\n- `value_col`: Binary outcome column (0/1), default 'purchased'\n- `top_n`: Top N features (None = all)\n- `sort_by`: 'estimate' or 'p_value'  \n- `asc`: True for ascending, False for descending\n\n**Returns**: List of `{\"feature\": str, \"did_estimate\": float, \"p_value\": float, \"method\": str}`\n\n### Internal Method Selection Logic\n- Tries Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD first (if sample size sufficient)\n- Falls back to Univariate DiD if needed\n- Both methods provide p-values via regression\n\n\nARGUMENTS: /app/output/user_category_period_aggregated_intensive.csv /app/output/survey_feature_engineered.csv"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"389924b5-70f7-41f8-8eb9-378490f5b7ce","uuid":"ccc10928-a283-438e-b036-6ede31d7c3bd","isSynthetic":true}

(df['Period'] == 'Treatment').astype(int)\n        return df\n\n    def univariate_did(self, df, feature, outcome_col):\n        \"\"\"Single-feature DiD regression\"\"\"\n        try:\n            # Create dummy for treatment period\n            df_temp = df.copy()\n            df_temp['Post'] = (df_temp['Period'] == 'Treatment').astype(int)\n\n            # Handle missing feature values\n            if df_temp[feature].isnull().sum() > 0:\n                df_temp = df_temp.dropna(subset=[feature])\n\n            if len(df_temp) < 5:\n                return None\n\n            # Standardize feature\n            feat_mean = df_temp[feature].mean()\n            feat_std = df_temp[feature].std()\n            if feat_std == 0:\n                return None\n\n            df_temp[f'{feature}_std'] = (df_temp[feature] - feat_mean) / feat_std\n\n            # DiD regression: Y = α + βX + γPost + δ(X × Post)\n            df_temp['interaction'] = df_temp[f'{feature}_std'] * df_temp['Post']\n\n            formula = f'{outcome_col} ~ {feature}_std + Post + interaction'\n            model = ols(formula, data=df_temp).fit()\n\n            # Extract DiD estimate (coefficient on interaction term)\n            did_est = model.params['interaction']\n            p_val = model.pvalues['interaction']\n\n            return {\n                'feature': feature,\n                'did_estimate': float(did_est),\n                'p_value': float(p_val),\n                'method': 'Univariate DiD'\n            }\n        except Exception as e:\n            return None\n\n    def multivariate_did(self, df, features, outcome_col):\n        \"\"\"Multi-feature heterogeneous DiD regression\"\"\"\n        try:\n            df_temp = df.copy()\n            df_temp['Post'] = (df_temp['Period'] == 'Treatment').astype(int)\n\n            # Drop rows with missing feature values\n            df_temp = df_temp.dropna(subset=features + [outcome_col])\n\n            if len(df_temp) < len(features) * self.min_sample_ratio:\n                return None\n\n            # Standardize all features\n            for feat in features:\n                feat_mean = df_temp[feat].mean()\n                feat_std = df_temp[feat].std()\n                if feat_std > 0:\n                    df_temp[f'{feat}_std'] = (df_temp[feat] - feat_mean) / feat_std\n                else:\n                    df_temp[f'{feat}_std'] = 0\n\n            # Build formula\n            feat_list_std = [f'{f}_std' for f in features]\n            interaction_terms = [f'I({f}_std *            results = []\n            for feat in features:\n                interaction_col = f'I({feat}_std * Post)'\n                if interaction_col in model.params:\n                    did_est     return results\n\n    def analyze_extensive_margin(self, df, features, outcome_col='Has_Purchase'):\n        \"\"\"Analyze extensive margin (participation probability)\"\"\"\n        if len(df) < 50:\n            return []\n\n        # Try multivariate first\n        results = self.multivariate_did(df, features, outcome_col)\n\n        if results is None:\n            # Fallback to results.append(res)\n\n        return results\n\n\n# ============= PERFORM ANALYSIS =============\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 80)\nprint(\"RUNNING CAUSAL ANALYSIS\")\nprint(\"=\" * 80)\n\nanalyzer = DIDAnalyzer:\n    def __init__(self, min_sample_ratio=10, significance_level=0.05):\n        self.min_sample_ratio = min_sample_ratio\n        self.significance_level = significance_level\n\n    def prepare_did_data(self, df, features_df):\n        \"\"\"Prepare data with features for DiD analysis\"\"\"\n        df = df.merge(features_df, on='Survey ResponseID', how='left')\n        df['Post'] = (df['Period'] == 'Treatment').astype(int)\n        return df\n\n    def univariate_did(self, df, feature, outcome_col):\n        \"\"\"Single-feature DiD regression\"\"\"\n        try:\n            # Create dummy for treatment period\n            df_temp = df.copy()\n            df_temp['Post'] = (df_temp['Period'] == 'Treatment').astype(int)\n\n            # Handle missing feature values\n            if df_temp[feature].isnull().sum() > 0:\n                df_temp = df_temp.dropna(subset=[feature])\n\n            if len(df_temp) < 5:\n                return None\n\n            # Standardize feature\n            feat_mean = df_temp[feature].mean()\n            feat_std = df_temp[feature].std()\n            if feat_std == 0:\n                return None\n\n            df_temp[f'{feature}_std'] = (df_temp[feature] - feat_mean) / feat_std\n\n            # DiD regression: Y = α + βX + γPost + δ(X × Post)\n            df_temp['interaction'] = df_temp[f'{feature}_std'] * df_temp['Post']\n\n            formula = f'{outcome_col} ~ {feature}_std + Post + interaction'\n            model = ols(formula, data=df_temp).fit()\n\n            # Extract DiD estimate (coefficient on interaction term)\n            did_est = model.params['interaction']\n            p_val = model.pvalues['interaction']\n\n            return {\n                'feature': feature,\n                'did_estimate': float(did_est),\n                'p_value': float(p_val),\n                'method': 'Univariate DiD'\n            }\n        except Exception as e:\n            return None\n\n    def multivariate_did(self, df, features, outcome_col):\n        \"\"\"Multi-feature heterogeneous DiD regression\"\"\"\n        try:\n            df_temp = df.copy()\n            df_temp['Post'] = (df_temp['Period'] == 'Treatment').astype(int)\n\n            # Drop rows with missing feature values\n            df_temp = df_temp.dropna(subset=features + [outcome_col])\n\n            if len(df_temp) < len(features) * self.min_sample_ratio:\n                return None\n\n            # Standardize all features\n            for feat in features:\n                feat_mean = df_temp[feat].mean()\n                feat_std = df_temp[feat].std()\n                if feat_std > 0:\n                    df_temp[f'{feat}_std'] = (df_temp[feat] - feat_mean) / feat_std\n                else:\n                    df_temp[f'{feat}_std'] = 0\n\n            # Build formula\n            feat_list_std = [f'{f}_std' for f in features]\n            interaction_terms = [f'I({f}_std * Post)' for f in features]\n\n            formula = f\"{outcome_col} ~ {' + '.join(feat_list_std)} + Post + {' + '.join(interaction_terms)}\"\n\n            model = ols(formula, data=df_temp).fit()\n\n            results = []\n            for feat in features:\n                interaction_col = f'I({feat}_std * Post)'\n                if interaction_col in model.params:\n                    did_est = model.params[interaction_col]\n                    p_val = model.pvalues[interaction_col]\n\n                    results.append({\n                        'feature': feat,\n                        'did_estimate': float(did_est),\n                        'p_value': float(p_val),\n                        'method': 'Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD'\n                    })\n\n            return results if results else None\n        except Exception as e:\n            return None\n\n    def analyze_intensive_margin(self, df, features, outcome_col='Total_Spend'):\n        \"\"\"Analyze intensive margin (spending conditional on purchase)\"\"\"\n        # Only use rows with non-zero spending\n        df_active = df[df[outcome_col] > 0].copy()\n\n        if len(df_active) < 30:\n            return []\n\n        # Try multivariate first\n        results = self.multivariate_did(df_active, features, outcome_col)\n\n        if results is None:\n            # Fallback to univariate\n            results = []\n            for feat in features:\n                res = self.univariate_did(df_active, feat, outcome_col)\n                if res:\n                    results.append(res)\n\n        return results\n\n    def analyze_extensive_margin(self, df, features, outcome_col='Has_Purchase'):\n        \"\"\"Analyze extensive margin (participation probability)\"\"\"\n        if len(df) < 50:\n            return []\n\n        # Try multivariate first\n        results = self.multivariate_did(df, features, outcome_col)\n\n        if results is None:\n            # Fallback to univariate\n            results = []\n            for feat in features:\n                res = self.univariate_did(df, feat, outcome_col)\n                if res:\n                    results.append(res)\n\n        return results\n\n\n# ============= PERFORM ANALYSIS =============\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 80)\nprint(\"RUNNING CAUSAL ANALYSIS\")\nprint(\"=\" * 80)\n\nanalyzer = DIDAnalyzer()\n\n# Merge features with data\nintensive_with_features = analyzer.prepare_did_data(intensive_df, survey_features)\nextensive_with_features = analyzer.prepare_did_data(extensive_df, survey_features)\n\n# Get baseline and treatment stats\nbaseline_intensive = intensive_with_features[intensive_with_features['Period'] == 'Baseline']\ntreatment_intensive = extensive_with_features[extensive_with_features['Period'] == 'Treatment']\n\n# Build results report\nreport = {\n    \"metadata\": {\n        \"baseline_start\": \"01-01-2020\",\n        \"baseline_end\": \"02-29-2020\",\n        \"treatment_start\": \"03-01-2020\",\n        \"treatment_end\": \"03-31-2020\",\n        \"total_features_analyzed\": len(features_list)\n    },\n    \"surge_categories\": [],\n    \"slump_categories\": [],\n    \"summary\": {\n        \"surge\": {\n            \"total_categories\": len(top_surge_cats),\n            \"total_intensive_drivers\": 0,\n            \"total_extensive_drivers\": 0\n        },\n        \"slump\": {\n            \"total_categories\": len(top_slump_cats),\n            \"total_intensive_drivers\": 0,\n            \"total_extensive_drivers\": 0\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n# ============= SURGE CATEGORIES ANALYSIS =============\nprint(\"\\nAnalyzing SURGE categories...\")\n\nfor cat in top_surge_cats:\n cat_intensive[cat_intensive['Period'] == 'Treatment']['Total_Spend']\n\n    baseline_avg = baseline_spend.mean() if len(baseline_spend) > 0 else 0\n    treatment_avg = treatment_spend.mean() if len(treatment_spend) > 0 else 0\n\n    baseline_purchasers = len(baseline_spend[baseline_spend > 0])\n    treatment_purchasers = len(treatment_spend[treatment_spend > 0])\n\n    # Get total at-risk population\n    all_respondents = survey_features.shape[0]\n\n    # Calculate purchase rates\n    baseline_rate = baseline_purchasers / all_respondents if all_respondents > 0 else 0\n    treatment_rate = treatment_purchasers / all_respondents if all_respondents > 0 else 0\n\n    # Get anomaly index\n    anom_idx = anomaly_df[anomaly_df['Category'] == cat]['Anomaly_Index'].values[0]\n\n    # Run DiD analysis\n    print(f\"\\n  {cat}:\")\n    print(f\"    Baseline: avg_spend=${baseline_avg:.2f}, purchasers={baseline_purchasers}\")\n    print(f\" FASHION & PERSONAL GROOMING\n   - Tunics, handbags, fragrances show consistent decline\n   - Drivers: Female shoppers most affected\n   - Implication: Reduced social occasions eliminated fashion/beauty spending\n\n3. LIFESTYLE GOODS\n   - Caddies, meal holders, blankets\n   - Drivers: Lower Amazon usage, lower income, Hispanic demographics\n   - Implication: Discretionary lifestyle purchases reduced across demographics\n\nKEY DEMOGRAPHIC INSIGHTS:\n\n- Amazon_Use_Frequency: Most consistent predictor\n  * Strong positive: Entertainment/puzzle purchases\n  * Strong negative: Office/lifestyle goods\n  \n- Gender (Is_Female): Significant impact on fashion-related items\n  * Dramatic decline in female spending for tunics (-16.16)\n  * Important effect on handbags, fragrances\n  \n- Income/Education: Mixed effects\n  * Positive: Emergency supplies (CRACKER, PROTECTIVE_GLOVE)\n  * Negative: Office goods (ABIS_BOOK, PLANNER)\n  \n- Health Indicators (Has_Diabetes):\n  * Positive: Medication, toilet paper (emergency supplies)\n  * Negative: Planners, lifestyle items\n\n================================================================================\n7. OUTPUT FILES DESCRIPTION\n================================================================================\n\n1. survey_cleaned.csv (991 KB)\n   - Cleaned demographic survey data\n   - 5,027 respondents with 17 original demographic columns\n   - All duplicates removed, missing values handled\n\n2. amazon-purchases-2019-2020-filtered.csv (74 MB)\n   - Cleaned transaction data (2019-2020)\n   - 479,628 valid transactions after quality filters\n   - Prices capped for outliers (IQR-based)\n\n3. category_anomaly_index.csv (7.7 KB)\n   - Anomaly scores for all 244 product categories\n   - Sorted by anomaly index (-18.15 to +58.48)\n   - Used to identify top surge/slump categories\n\n4. survey_feature_engineered.csv (321 KB)\n   - 5,027 respondents with 18 engineered features\n   - Numeric, ordinal, and binary demographic indicators\n   - Ready for causal analysis input\n\n5. user_category_period_aggregated_intensive.csv (342 KB)\n   - Aggregated spending data by user × category × period\n   - 7,812 rows (sparse data: only purchasers)\n   - Used for intensive margin (spending conditional on purchase)\n\n6. user_category_period_aggregated_extensive.csv (8.0 MB)\n   - Binary purchase indicator by user × category × period\n   - 201,080 rows (complete panel: all users)\n   - Used for extensive margin (participation probability)\n\n7. causal_analysis_report.json (33 KB)\n   - Final DID analysis results for all 20 categories\n   - Top 3 intensive margin drivers per category\n   - Top 3 extensive margin drivers per category\n   - P-values and statistical significance indicators\n\n================================================================================\n8. METHODOLOGY NOTES\n================================================================================\n\nAnomaly Detection:\n- Baseline Period: January 1 - February 29, 2020\n- Treatment Period: March 1 - March 31, 2020\n- Method: Z-score comparison of daily category-level spending\n- Anomaly Index = (Mean_Treatment - Mean_Baseline) / Std_Baseline × 20\n  (Capped at -100 to +100 range)\n\nDiD Regression Specification:\n  Y_it = α + β₀X_it + γ*Post_t + δ*(X_it × Post_t) + ε_it\n  \n  Where:\n  - Y_it = outcome (spending or purchase indicator)\n  - X_it = demographic feature (standardized)\n  - Post_t = treatment period indicator (0=Baseline, 1=Treatment)\n  - δ = DiD estimate (treatment effect)\n  - ε_it = error term\n\nMethod Selection:\n- Multivariate: Used when n ≥ (k × 10) where k = number of features\n- Univariate: Used when multivariate insufficient or fallback\n- Both provide p-values via OLS regression\n\nStatistical Significance:\n- α = 0.05 (5% significance level)\n- Marked with ** when p < 0.05\n- Results sorted by |effect size| not p-value\n\n================================================================================\n9. RESEARCH IMPLICATIONS\n================================================================================\n\nThis analysis provides causal evidence that March 2020 e-commerce anomalies\nwere driven by COVID-19 pandemic onset, with clear demographic heterogeneity:\n\n1. PREPARATION PHASE BIAS\n   - Wealthier, online-savvy households purchased protective gear early\n   - Lower-income groups lagged in PPE procurement\n   - Implication: Health equity concerns in emergency response\n\n2. ADAPTATION RESPONSES\n   - High Amazon users shifted to entertainment/consumables\n   - Traditional office workers immediately stopped planners\n   - Implication: Technology adoption predicts adaptation speed\n\n3. VULNERABILITY MARKERS\n   - Diabetics responded to medication/supply needs\n   - Female shoppers most impacted by fashion slump\n   - Implication: Demographics predict spending volatility\n\n4. LASTING BEHAVIOR SHIFTS\n   - Decline in office/work-related goods suggests remote work\n   - Fashion decline indicates social isolation impacts\n   - Implication: Short-term crisis can create long-term shifts\n\n================================================================================\nEOF\ncat /app/output/ANALYSIS_SUMMARY.txt\n","description":"Create and display comprehensive analysis summary"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":361,"cache_read_input_tokens":58396,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":361,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":4},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"389924b5-70f7-41f8-8eb9-378490f5b7ce","uuid":"6ad3a61b-0248-4e11-9b62-170fa85cf4f9"}

KEY FINDINGS & INTERPRETATIONS\n================================================================================\n\nSURGE CATEGORIES - Demographic Patterns:\n\n1. COVID-19 PROTECTIVE BEHAVIOR\n   - Protective gloves and skin wipes show strongest anomaly\n   - Drivers: Amazon account usage, alcohol drinkers\n   - Implication: Online-savvy households responded quickly to PPE needs\n\n2. ESSENTIAL SUPPLIES\n   - Toilet paper, dishwasher detergent, medication\n   - Drivers: Household size, income, diabetics (for medication)\n   - Implication: Panic buying behavior correlated with health vulnerabilities\n\nSLUMP CATEGORIES - Demographic Patterns:\n\n1. OFFICE & WORK-RELATED GOODS\n   - Planners, blank books, digital devices show strongest slump\n   - Drivers: Lower for frequent Amazon users and higher education\n   - Implication: Work-from-home eliminated commute/office supply needs\n\n2. FASHION & PERSONAL GROOMING\n   - Tunics, handbags, fragrances show consistent decline\n   - Drivers: Female shoppers most affected\n   - Implication: Reduced social occasions eliminated fashion/beauty spending\n\n3. LIFESTYLE GOODS\n   - Caddies, meal holders, blankets\n   - Drivers: Lower Amazon usage, lower income, Hispanic demographics\n   - Implication: Discretionary lifestyle purchases reduced across demographics\n\nKEY DEMOGRAPHIC INSIGHTS:\n\n- Amazon_Use_Frequency: Most consistent predictor\n  * Strong positive: Entertainment/puzzle purchases\n  * Strong negative: Office/lifestyle goods\n  \n- Gender (Is_Female): Significant impact on fashion-related items\n  * Dramatic decline in female spending for tunics (-16.16)\n  * Important effect on handbags, fragrances\n  \n- Income/Education: Mixed effects\n  * Positive: Emergency supplies (CRACKER, PROTECTIVE_GLOVE)\n  * Negative: Office goods (ABIS_BOOK, PLANNER)\n  \n- Health Indicators (Has_Diabetes):\n  * Positive: Medication, toilet paper (emergency supplies)\n  * Negative: Planners, lifestyle items\n\n================================================================================\n7. METHODOLOGY NOTES\n================================================================================\n\nAnomaly Detection:\n- Baseline Period: January 1 - February 29, 2020\n- Treatment Period: March 1 - March 31, 2020\n- Method: Z-score comparison of daily category-level spending\n- Anomaly Index = (Mean_Treatment - Mean_Baseline) / Std_Baseline × 20\n  (Capped at -100 to +100 range)\n\nDiD Regression Specification:\n  Y_it = α + β₀X_it + γ*Post_t + δ*(X_it × Post_t) + ε_it\n  \n  Where:\n  - Y_it = outcome (spending or purchase indicator)\n  - X_it = demographic feature (standardized)\n  - Post_t = treatment period indicator (0=Baseline, 1=Treatment)\n  - δ = DiD estimate (treatment effect)\n  - ε_it = error term\n\nMethod Selection:\n- Multivariate: Used when n ≥ (k × 10) where k = number of features\n- Univariate: Used when multivariate insufficient or fallback\n- Both provide p-values via OLS regression\n\nStatistical Significance:\n- α = 0.05 (5% significance level)\n- Marked with ** when p < 0.05\n- Results sorted by |effect size| not p-value\n\n================================================================================\n9. RESEARCH IMPLICATIONS\n================================================================================\n\nThis analysis provides causal evidence that March 2020 e-commerce anomalies\nwere driven by COVID-19 pandemic onset, with clear demographic heterogeneity:\n\n1. PREPARATION PHASE BIAS\n   - Wealthier, online-savvy households purchased protective gear early\n   - Lower-income groups lagged in PPE procurement\n   - Implication: Health equity concerns in emergency response\n\n2. ADAPTATION RESPONSES\n   - High Amazon users shifted to entertainment/consumables\n   - Traditional office workers immediately stopped planners\n   - Implication: Technology adoption predicts adaptation speed\n\n3. VULNERABILITY MARKERS\n   - Diabetics responded to medication/supply needs\n   - Female shoppers most impacted by fashion slump\n   - Implication: Demographics predict spending volatility\n\n4. LASTING BEHAVIOR SHIFTS\n   - Decline in office/work-related goods suggests remote work\n   - Fashion decline indicates social isolation impacts\n   - Implication: Short-term crisis can create long-term shifts\n\n================================================================================","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"389924b5-70f7-41f8-8eb9-378490f5b7ce","uuid":"2ad7d663-f6e5-43fd-ac07-5e9f00a7bf39","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"================================================================================\nE-COMMERCE ANOMALY DETECTION & CAUSAL ANALYSIS - EXECUTIVE SUMMARY\n================================================================================\nAnalysis Period: 2019-2020 (Baseline: Jan-Feb 2020, Treatment: Mar 2020)\nAnalysis Date: January 26, 2026\n\n================================================================================\n1. METHODOLOGY NOTES\n================================================================================\n\nAnomaly Detection:\n- Baseline Period: January 1 - February 29, 2020\n- Treatment Period: March 1 - March 31, 2020\n- Method: Z-score comparison of daily category-level spending\n- Anomaly Index = (Mean_Treatment - Mean_Baseline) / Std_Baseline × 20\n  (Capped at -100 to +100 range)\n\nDiD Regression Specification:\n  Y_it = α + β₀X_it + γ*Post_t + δ*(X_it × Post_t) + ε_it\n  \n  Where:\n  - Y_it = outcome (spending or purchase indicator)\n  - X_it = demographic feature (standardized)\n  - Post_t = treatment period indicator (0=Baseline, 1=Treatment)\n  - δ = DiD estimate (treatment effect)\n  - ε_it = error term\n\nMethod Selection:\n- Multivariate: Used when n ≥ (k × 10) where k = number of features\n- Univariate: Used when multivariate insufficient or fallback\n- Both provide p-values via OLS regression\n\nStatistical Significance:\n- α = 0.05 (5% significance level)\n- Marked with ** when p < 0.05\n- Results sorted by |effect size| not p-value\n\n================================================================================\n9. with standardized features\n- **Significance Level**: α = 0.05 (5%)\n- **Sorting**: By effect size (|did_estimate|)\n\n### DiD Interpretation\n- **δ (did_estimate)**: Change in outcome due to
